Custom smooth objective:
```
def smooth_obj_func(x, n, alpha):
    dx=2/n
    c=corr(self?)
    m=c.max()
    r=alpha*(c-m)
    r-max? mx = r.max() (0 maybe)
    lse = mx + log(sum(exp(r-mx)))
    return dx*(m + lse/alpha)
```
Grad:
```
def smooth_grad(x,n,alpha):
    c=...
    m=c.max()
    r=alpha*(c-m)
    r = r - r.max()
    w = exp(r); w /= w.sum()
    # grad = dx * jacT(w)
    return dx * jacT(w,x,n)
```

Projection exact:
```
def project_sum_box(x, n):
    target=n/2
    # if sum close, return clip
    x=np.clip(x,0,1)
    # If sum == target maybe
    for many loops? Use bisection on mu for sum (x+mu clipped). Need find mu such that sum clipped(x+mu)=target.
    lo=-x.min()-1e? Actually if mu -> -∞ sum 0, mu -> ∞ sum n. sum monotone.
    lo = -1.0? Since x in [0,1], x+mu ≤1 when mu≤1-x. For target between 0,n, mu between -1 and 1. Bisection 60 iters.
    def ssum(mu): return (np.clip(x+mu,0,1)).sum()
    lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
    for _ in range(80): mid=(lo+hi)/2; if ssum(mid)<target: lo=mid else hi=mid
    y=clip(x+hi,0,1)
    # correct residual by adjusting unfixed points
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       mask=(y>eps)&(y<1-eps); if any: y[mask]+=diff/mask.sum(); clip
    return y
```

### Exact projection
```
def proj_simplex(x, n):
    x = np.asarray(x, float)
    # If already sum close after clip
    x = np.clip(x,0,1)
    s = x.sum()
    target = 0.5*n
    if abs(s-target) < 1e-13:
        # fix ties? return
        return x
    lo = -1.0
    hi = 1.0
    # ensure sum lo<target<hi (after clip values in [0,1])
    for _ in range(80):
        mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
        y=np.clip(x+mid,0,1)
        if y.sum() < target:
            lo=mid
        else:
            hi=mid
    y=np.clip(x+hi,0,1)
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       mask=(y>1e-9)&(y<1-1e-9)
       if mask.any():
          y[mask]+=diff/mask.sum()
          y=np.clip(y,0,1)
       else:
          # adjust largest non-bound
          idx=int(np.argmax((y-1e-9)*(1-1e-9)))? 
          y[idx]=np.clip(y[idx]+diff,0,1)
    return y
```
80 iters * n (500) =40k ops per projection; if many projections maybe okay. Could reduce to 50.

But for CD we might call project per pass, not per coordinate.

### compute c5
```
def compute_c5(h,n):
    c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
    return float(c.max())*(2.0/n)
```
Precompute maybe cmax.

### smooth objective and gradient
Need alpha parameter. Potential overflow: r=alpha*(c-cmax); since c-cmax≤0, r≤0. If alpha large (1e6), fine values ≤0. `np.exp(r)` underflows for large negative; okay. Weights sum maybe 0? If all underflow? r max=0, at least one exp=1, sum≥1. Good.
```
def smooth_p(h,n,alpha):
    c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
    m=c.max()
    r=alpha*(c-m)
    # r already <=0; max 0; for safety r -= r.max()
    w=np.exp(r)
    w /= w.sum()
    return w, m, c
```
But gradient needs c? only w. For objective, return dx*(m + log(sum e^r)/alpha).
```
def smooth_obj_grad(x,n,alpha):
    c=correlate
    m=c.max()
    r=alpha*(c-m)
    # maybe if m? fine
    lw=np.exp(r)
    z=lw.sum()
    val=(m + (np.log(z))/alpha)*(2.0/n)
    grad_w=lw/z
    y=1.0-x
    A=np.convolve(y, grad_w, 'full')
    C=np.convolve(x, grad_w[::-1], 'full')
    idx=n-1
    g=(A-C)[idx:idx+n]*(2.0/n)
    return val, g
```
Need ensure grad_w length = 2n-1. Convolve y (n) with w (2n-1): full length 3n-2? Wait y length n, w length 2n-1, full length 3n-2. Slice [n-1 : n-1+n] length n. Good. For C: x length n, w_rev length 2n-1, full length 3n-2; slice same. Good.
Let's test gradient with finite difference mentally maybe. For n=2 earlier worked. But is A = conv(y,w) or conv(w,y)? Convolution commutative. A_j = ∑ y_i w_{n-1+j-i}. `np.convolve(y,w)[n-1+j] = ∑_i y_i w_{n-1+j-i}` if w index from 0. Yes.
C_j = ∑_i x_i w_{n-1+i-j}. We used convolve(x, w[::-1])[n-1+j]. Let's verify formula in general using n=3 maybe. We can trust.
Note: y=1-x. But for diagonal, A-C gives (1-x_i)w_diag - x_i w_diag =1-2x_i. Good.

SLSQP with jac:
```
def slsqp_smooth(h,n,alphas,maxiter,deadline):
    best=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(best,n)
    for alpha in alphas:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       def obj(x): return smooth_obj(x,n,alpha)
       def jac(x): return smooth_grad(x,n,alpha)
       cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum_con,'jac':sum_con_jac,'args':(n,)}]
       bounds=[(0,1)]*n
       try:
          res=minimize(obj,best,jac=jac,args=(n,alpha)?, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol':1e-18})
       except:
          continue
       new=proj_simplex(res.x,n)
       newc=compute_c5(new,n)
       if newc<best_c: best,...
    return best,best_c
```
Inside function top-level no closures? Problem says no closures or lambdas. We cannot define obj/jac nested. We need top-level functions that take n,alpha. But minimize requires fun(x, *args); can pass args. Top-level:
```
def smooth_obj_only(x,n,alpha): ...
def smooth_grad_only(x,n,alpha): ...
def sum_con(x,n): return x.sum()-n/2
def sum_con_jac(x,n): return np.ones_like(x)
```
Then call `minimize(smooth_obj_only, best, args=(n,alpha), jac=smooth_grad_only, ...)`. Good.

SLSQP expects jac returns gradient. Good.

Potential issue SLSQP with 500 variables and constraints may be slow; but we can use restricted maxiter. We can also use `method='L-BFGS-B'` with penalty to enforce sum: objective + mu*(sum-target)^2. Maybe faster. Let's consider.

Projected gradient (logsumexp) with exact projection:
```
def proj_grad_step(h,n,alphas,steps,deadline,lr_schedule):
   h=proj
   for alpha in alphas:
      for it in range(steps):
         val,grad=smooth_obj_grad(h,n,alpha)
         # simple line search backtracking on projected space: try lr values
         best_h=h; best_val=val
         for lr in lrs:
             cand=proj(h-lr*grad,n)
             cv=compute_c5(cand,n) # or smooth val? Use true C for monotone? Smooth approx not monotone if changing. Use compute_c5.
             if cv<best_val: ...
        if not improved: break
        h=best_h
```
But each line test compute c O(n^2); lrs 5*iterations many. Could be expensive. Instead use L-BFGS-B with sum penalty and bounds (no projection) perhaps.

L-BFGS-B with penalty:
```
def penal_obj(x,n,alpha,pen): smooth_p + pen*(sum-target)^2
grad = smooth_grad + 2*pen*(sum-target)*ones
res=minimize(penal_obj,x,method='L-BFGS-B',bounds,bounds large)
```
Then project. This may converge faster than SLSQP. We can use multiple pen increasing (1e2,1e4,1e6). But L-BFGS-B no equality; large pen might make gradient dominated and slow. However sums from SLSQP already feasible. Could use for starts.

Maybe use scipy.optimize.minimize method 'SLSQP' with custom jac is enough.

Thus exact c update for sparse deltas d at set P:
1. For each p in P:
   - c[p:p+n] += d_p * (1 - h_rev_old)

   - c[n-1] -= d_p^2
2. For each ordered pair (a,b) in P, a≠b:
   - c[n-1 + a - b] -= d_a * d_b

Hill climb:
```
def try_flip(c, h, i,j,cmax):
   c2=c.copy()
   # update for i 1->0, j 0->1
   omhr = 1.0 - h[::-1]
   # d_i=-1
   c2[i:i+n] -= omhr
   c2[n-1-i:n-1-i+n] += h
   # d_j=+1
   c2[j:j+n] += omhr
   c2[n-1-j:n-1-j+n] -= h
   c2[n-1] -= 2.0
   c2[n-1+i-j] += 1.0
   c2[n-1+j-i] += 1.0
   return float(c2.max())
```

Could also use `method='L-BFGS-B'` with penalty before SLSQP. L-BFGS-B much faster for smooth objective. Let's implement a simple penalty L-BFGS-B for polishing:
```
def penal_obj(x,n,alpha,pen):
    val,grad_base = smooth_obj_grad(x,n,alpha)
    s = x.sum()-n/2
    return val + 0.5*pen*s*s
def penal_grad(...):
    val,grad = smooth_obj_grad(...)
    s = x.sum()-n/2
    grad = grad + pen*s*np.ones_like(x)
    return grad
```
Then `minimize(penal_obj, x, jac=penal_grad, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':..., 'ftol':...})`. Large pen (1e4-1e6). L-BFGS-B no sum constraint but reduces penalty. Then project. This can be fast. Need top-level smooth_obj_grad. But penal_obj calls smooth_obj_grad and returns val. For minimize with jac=penal_grad, need separate args. Top-level:
```
def penal_obj_only(x,n,alpha,pen): return smooth_obj_grad(x,n,alpha)[0] + ...
def penal_grad_only(x,n,alpha,pen): val,grad=smooth_obj_grad(...); ...
```
Could run after SLSQP or instead. But too many functions.

Maybe SLSQP with custom jac is sufficient and simpler.

### Coordinate descent improvement details
Implementation:
```

          best_x=old; best_val= c.max()? But c is current c; if we update h, c not updated. We can evaluate candidates using c and old h. If accept, update c and h/omhr, best_val candidate, improved true.
          # grid: coarse = np.linspace(0,1,G) perhaps include old if not. G=24.
          # Evaluate only x values that differ enough.
          for x in candidates:
             d=x-old
             if abs(d)<1e-12: val=best_val
             else:
                c2=c.copy(); c2[p:p+n]+=d*omhr; c2[n-1-p:n-1-p+n]-=d*h; c2[n-1]-=d*d; val=c2.max()
             if val < best_val - tol: best...
          if best_x != old:

       h=proj_simplex(h,n)
       # after projection, recompute c
       newc=compute_c5(h,n)
       if newc < best_c: best_c = newc
       if not improved: break
    return h,best_c
```

### Binarization and binary local search with c update exact
Let's detail binary functions.
```
def binarize(h,n):
    if n % 2: return None
    target=n//2
    # choose top target values; for ties, deterministic via index hash? Use argsort? n small
    idx = np.argpartition(h, target-1)[:target] if target>0 else []
    b=np.zeros(n,float); b[idx]=1.0
    # if target 0/ n no; handle
    return b
```
But if h has exactly fractional top with duplicates? argpartition fine. Sum target.
Binary local search:
```
def binary_polish(b,n,deadline,rand_seed,max_trials=None):
   h=b.copy()
   c=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')
   cmax=float(c.max())
   omhr=1.0-h[::-1]
   best_h=h.copy(); best_cmax=cmax
   t_end=deadline
   # hill climb loop
   iterations=0
   no_improve=0
   while time.time()<t_end:
      # choose random one and zero
      i=rng.randint(n)
      while h[i] < 0.5: i=... (limit)
      j=rng.randint(n)
      while h[j] > 0.5: j=...
      if i==j: continue
      c2=c.copy()
      # updates as above
      newmax=float(c2.max())
      if newmax < cmax - 1e-12:
          c=c2; cmax=newmax
          h[i]=0; h[j]=1
          omhr[n-1-i]=1.0; omhr[n-1-j]=0.0
          if cmax < best_cmax: best...
          no_improve=0
          # After several improvements maybe recompute? not needed; but if floating drift? int exact.
      else:
          no_improve +=1
          # if many no improve, maybe perturb or break
          if no_improve > 2000: break
      iterations+=1
   return best_h,best_cmax/dx
```
If no improve > threshold, local optimum. Then we can perturb:
```
def perturb(h,n,rng,k):
   for _ in range(k): choose one i zero j, flip (update c maybe) even if worse
   return h,c,cmax,omhr
```
Then hill climb again. To save code, maybe not.

def continuous_improve(h,n,deadline,hard=False):
   h=proj
   # phase 1: L-BFGS-B penalty
   for alpha,pen in [(100,1e2),(1000,1e4),(10000,1e5),(50000,1e6)]: if time...

### Customized coordinate CD with exact update and grid
Let's write efficient function.
```
def coord_cd(h, n, max_passes=10, deadline=None, grid=32, tol=1e-12):
    h = proj_simplex(np.asarray(h,float), n).copy()
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
    dx=2.0/n
    best_c = float(c.max())*dx
    for _ in range(max_passes):
        if deadline is not None and time.time() > deadline: break
        omhr = 1.0 - h[::-1]
        improved = False
        # maybe randomize order each pass to avoid cycles? Use fixed or rng? We can use a deterministic permutation based on pass.
        order = np.arange(n)
        # maybe shuffle with rng? But coord_cd no rng parameter; can use global? Keep fixed.
        for p in order:
            old = h[p]
            current_max = float(c.max())  # c is current. Calling max each coordinate O(n) maybe okay.
            best_val = current_max
            best_x = old
            # coarse candidates
            # We can skip if old at 0/1? No, allow.
            # candidates: old, 0,1
            xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, grid)
            # evaluate maybe only if abs(x-old)>1e-8
            for x0 in xs:
                x = float(x0)
                d = x - old
                if abs(d) < 1e-13:
                    val = best_val
                else:
                    c2 = c.copy()
                    c2[p:p+n] += d * omhr
                    c2[n-1-p:n-1-p+n] -= d * h
                    c2[n-1] -= d*d
                    val = float(c2.max())
                if val < best_val - tol:
                    best_val = val; best_x = x
            if best_x != old:
                # refine around best_x
                # spacing grid/(grid-1)
                step0 = 1.0/(grid-1) if grid>1 else 1.0
                lo = max(0.0, best_x - step0)
                hi = min(1.0, best_x + step0)
                for x0 in np.linspace(lo, hi, 16):
                    x=float(x0); d=x-old
                    if abs(d)<1e-13: continue
                    c2=c.copy(); ...
                    val=float(c2.max())
                    if val < best_val - tol: best...
                # accept if best_x changed
                d=best_x-old
                if abs(d)>1e-13:
                   c[p:p+n] += d*omhr
                   c[n-1-p:n-1-p+n] -= d*h
                   c[n-1] -= d*d
                   h[p]=best_x
                   omhr[n-1-p]=1.0-best_x
                   improved=True
        # after pass, project sum and update c
        h = proj_simplex(h, n)
        c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
        cur = float(c.max())*dx
        if cur < best_c - tol:
            best_c = cur
        if not improved:
            break
    # ensure best_c compute
    c = np.correlate(h,1-h,'full'); best_c=float(c.max())*dx
    return h, best_c
```
This recomputes `c.max()` each coordinate (O(1000)), fine. But `c.copy()` and max for each candidate. For grid=32+16=48 candidates, 500 preds ->24k candidates per pass; each copy 1000 ->24M copy, plus slice updates 24M*? Actually 24k* (copy1000 + 2*500) =48M floats, maybe ~0.1s? In Python loop 24k, okay maybe 2-5s/pass. Too much for many passes.
Could reduce grid: Coarse 16 + refine 8 =24 candidates. For n=500, 12k per pass, 24M floats, ~1s. Good. Use grid=16 for usual, final grid=40 maybe one pass. Since gap tiny, 16 might miss; but SLSQP already. For final polish, use grid=32 for one pass if time.

Can we vectorize candidate evaluations for a coordinate to reduce Python loop? We can create matrix for affected? But c copy per candidate maybe. We can precompute for slices values across d? For fixed p, c2 for candidate d:
- base c. The two slice updates are linear in d, point update -d^2. The max of entire c2. We could compute for all d candidates simultaneously:
   Create `D` shape (G). `base` c shape L. For first slice, add outer? Need c2 = c[None,:] + d[:,None]*coeff_first? But coeff only nonzero on slice. We can build `base_updates` arrays length L (omhr and h placements) and compute `vals = c[None,:] + D[:,None]*coeff - D[:,None]^2*diag`. Then max over axis=1. That is O(G*L) in C, no Python loop, but memory G*L (16*1000=16k) fine. For each coordinate: build coeff length L zeros, fill two slices with ±omhr, ±h, diag -1. Then `cand = base_c[None,:] + D[:,None]*coeff - (D[:,None]**2)*diag`? Diag only at m=n-1: subtract D^2. Then `maxes = cand.max(axis=1)`. This evaluates all candidates vectorized. Much faster! Need construct coeff each coordinate O(L) but okay.
Let's detail:
For coordinate p old. We want candidates x array length K, d=x-old.
Coefficient A_m for linear change:
- For m in [p, p+n): A += (1 - h_rev) for those positions. h_rev sequence? For m index offset t = m-p from 0..n-1, h_rev[t]=h[n-1-t]. So A[p:p+n] += one_minus_h_rev_full? `omhr = 1 - h[::-1]` length n; A[p:p+n] = omhr.
- For m in [n-1-p, n-1-p+n): A -= h (full h). (Because second linear term -d*h, included in A; update c + d*A)
At diag m=n-1, A has both sum. Quadratic B_m=1 at diag, 0 else; update -d^2*B.
Then for each d: c_new_m = c_m + d*A_m - d^2*B_m.
Vectorized:
```
A = np.zeros(L); A[p:p+n] = omhr; A[start:start+n] -= h; B = np.zeros(L); B[n-1]=1.0
cand = c[None,:] + d[:,None]*A[None,:] - (d[:,None]**2)*B[None,:]
maxs = cand.max(axis=1)
```
Memory KxL; K=24,L=1000 ->24000 floats 192KB per coordinate; allocate each coordinate 500 -> maybe overhead but okay. We can avoid B array by subtracting d^2 at diag: `cand[:, n-1] -= d*d`. But cand is 2D; `cand -= d[:,None]*A[None,:]` in place? Let's optimize:
```
base = c[None,:] * np.ones? allocate cand = c.reshape(1,-1) + d[:,None]*A[None,:]
cand[:, n-1] -= d*d
maxs = cand.max(axis=1)
```

Candidate grid: We can create `xs = np.linspace(0,1,K)`; d=xs-old. Also maybe include old exactly and 0,1 if K includes endpoints. Good. For refinement, around best_x: create 12 points.
For accept, update c via slices: `c[p:p+n] += d*omhr`; `c[start:start+n] -= d*h`; `c[n-1]-=d*d`. Good.

This vectorized coordinate candidate might allow more grid (e.g., 64) per coordinate. K=64,L=1000 =>64k; 500 coords =>32M elements processed per pass, okay. But allocation per coordinate 500*64k =32M floats total per pass, ~256MB memory allocated over time; okay. Could still be ~1s/pass. Good.

We can avoid building full A zeros each coordinate by using base c copy and slice additions? Vectorized with A requires fill zeros L + two slices. Fine. Could preallocate A and set to zero? Vectorized candidate function:
```
def coord_candidate_max(c, A, d, n):
    # d 1D K
    cand = c + np.outer(d, A)  # broadcasting c shape (1,L)
    diag = n-1
    cand[:, diag] -= d*d
    return cand.max(axis=1)
```
`np.outer(d,A)` KxL. Or `c[None,:] + d[:,None]*A[None,:]`. This computes K*L multiplications. Could be heavy but C.

### Smooth objective and gradient with convolution exact
Let's verify gradient formula with convolution thoroughly.
Define c_m = ∑_i h_{i+τ}(1-h_i), m=τ+n-1.
Derivative wrt h_j:
First (h_j as first): contributions for i = j-τ ∈ [0,n-1] => τ = j-i, m=n-1+j-i. Sum over i=0..n-1 of (1-h_i) w_{n-1+j-i}. This is `np.convolve(1-h, w, 'full')[n-1+j]`? Let y=1-h. Convolution c_s=∑_{i} y_i w_{s-i}. Set s=n-1+j. Then term y_i w_{n-1+j-i}. Sum i=0..n-1. But w index must be 0..2n-2. s-i ranges n-1+j to j. This includes positive only? For i>n-1+j negative? Convolution sum only valid w≥0; those terms ignored. But our sum over all i includes m=j..n-1+j all valid 0..2n-2? j≥0 so m≥j≥0; n-1+j≤2n-2. All valid. Convolution with y_i for i where m < n-1? Actually if s=n-1+j, i from ? For i=0, m=n-1+j; for i=n-1, m=j. All valid. So equal to sum. Good.
Second (h_j as second): contributions for i=j, first index k=j+τ, τ=k-j, m=n-1+k-j, k=0..n-1. Sum -h_k w_{n-1+k-j}. We used C_j = convolve(h, w[::-1])[n-1+j]. Let wr_m = w_{L-1-m}, L=2n-1. Conv = ∑_k h_k wr_{s-k} with s=n-1+j = ∑ h_k w_{L-1-(s-k)} = ∑ h_k w_{2n-2 - n +1 -j + k} = ∑ h_k w_{n-1 -j + k}. Yes. For k=0..n-1, indices n-1-j..2n-2-j? At k=n-1 index 2n-2-j valid? Wait original second m = n-1+k-j ranges n-1-j (≥0) to n-1+n-1-j = 2n-2-j (≤2n-2). But c_m length max 2n-2. Good. Original sum k=0..n-1 all valid? For j small, m high; j large, low. Yes. So gradient = A - C. Good.

Check for n=2 earlier worked. Good.

Convolve with `np.convolve` direct for n up to 1000 maybe O(n*2n). `smooth_grad` called many times; could be bottleneck. We can use FFT for n large? `scipy.signal.fftconvolve` not listed? scipy yes. But direct for 500 is small. For n=1000, convolve length 500*1999~1M; still okay. Use `np.convolve`.

### SLSQP and L-BFGS-B time
Maybe implement a generic `scipy_smooth_minimize` with method. But no closures. We'll create two functions:
```
def slsqp_polish(h,n,alphas,maxiter,deadline):
   h=proj; best_c=compute_c5
   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
   cons=...
   for alpha in alphas:
      if time>deadline: break
      try:
         res=minimize(smooth_obj_only,best, args=(n,alpha), jac=smooth_grad_only, args=..., method='SLSQP', constraints=cons, bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12})
      except Exception: pass
      new=proj(res.x,n); newc=compute_c5(...); if newc<best_c: ...
   return best_h,best_c
```
Note `minimize` fun and jac both take args; `fun=smooth_obj_only, args=(n,alpha), jac=smooth_grad_only, args=...` same. In scipy, if `jac=True` it calls fun? We provide function. Good.

def lbfgs_polish(h,n,alphas,pen,deadline,maxiter):

   bounds=[(0,1)]*n
   for alpha in alphas:
       res=minimize(penal_obj_only,best,args=(n,alpha,pen), jac=penal_grad_only, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12})
       new=proj(res.x,n); if c improve...
   return
```

def smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha):
   c=...; m=...; r=...; ww=exp; z=sum; val=...; grad=...
   return val,grad
```

def smooth_obj_only(x,n,alpha): return smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)[0]

```
This recomputes if both called separately; SLSQP/L-BFGS call separately anyway. But inside each, fine. `penal_obj_only` can call `smooth_val_grad` and add penalty.

### Projected random restarts
Since we want different than previous, include random perturbations around best with SLSQP? Previous did. We can do a few:

### Smoother objective logsumexp stable
`r = alpha * (c - m)`. Since c-m ≤0, max r=0. But due float, maybe positive 1e-12; do `r -= r.max()`.

For alpha very large, objective almost max but gradient weights maybe one. SLSQP may handle.

### Coordinate CD vectorized implementation details

```
def coord_cd(h, n, max_passes=5, deadline=None, grid=32, refine=12, tol=1e-11):
    h = proj_simplex(h, n).astype(float, copy=True)
    dx = 2.0 / n
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    best_c = float(c.max()) * dx
    L = 2*n - 1
    for p_ in range(max_passes):
        if deadline is not None and time.time() > deadline: break
        h_cur = h # already feasible
        c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, 'full')
        omhr = 1.0 - h[::-1]
        improved = False
        A = np.zeros(L)
        # Could preallocate A outside p loop and reset each p.
        for p in range(n):
            old = float(h[p])
            A.fill(0.0)
            A[p:p+n] += omhr
            st = n - 1 - p
            A[st:st+n] -= h
            # coarse candidates
            xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, grid)
            d = xs - old
            # If grid includes old? maybe not. Add old.
            # Evaluate vectorized

            # But c shape (L,), broadcasting: c[None,:] + d[:,None]*A[None,:]
            maxs = (c + np.outer(d, A)).max(axis=1)
            # diag correction
            # np.outer returns KxL. We can subtract d*d from diag column:
            # cand = ... (already)
```

```

```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

def project_simplex(h,n): ...
def compute_c5(h,n): ...
def smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha): ...
def smooth_obj(x,n,alpha): return smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)[0]
def smooth_grad(x,n,alpha): return smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)[1]
def sum_con(x,n): return x.sum()-0.5*n
def sum_con_jac(x,n): return np.ones_like(x)
def penal_obj(x,n,alpha,pen):
   val,grad=smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha); s=x.sum()-0.5*n; return val+0.5*pen*s*s
def penal_grad(x,n,alpha,ped):
   val,grad=smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha); s=x.sum()-0.5*n; return grad+pen*s*np.ones_like(x)
```
Need shadow variable names.

```
def slsqp_polish(h,n,alphas,maxiter,deadline):
   h = np.array(h,float).copy(); h=project_simplex(h,n)
   best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
   cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum_con,'jac':sum_con_jac,'args':(n,)}]
   for al in alphas:
      if time.time()>deadline: break
      try:
         res=minimize(smooth_obj, h, args=(n,al), jac=smooth_grad, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12})
      except Exception: continue
      nh=project_simplex(res.x,n); nc=compute_c5(nh,n)
      if nc < best_c:
         h=nh; best_c=nc
   return h,best_c
```
`smooth_grad` without args? It expects x,n,alpha. Good.

   cand[:, n-1] -= d*d

### Binary functions code
```
def binarize(h,n):
    if n % 2 != 0: return None
    target=n//2
    if target <=0 or target >= n: ...
    idx=np.argpartition(h, target-1)[:target]
    b=np.zeros(n,float); b[idx]=1.0
    return b

def binary_trial(c,h,omhr,i,j,n):
    c2=c.copy()
    s1=i; e1=i+n
    c2[s1:e1] -= omhr
    s2=n-1-i; e2=s2+n
    c2[s2:e2] += h
    c2[j:j+n] += omhr
    s3=n-1-j; e3=s3+n
    c2[s3:e3] -= h
    c2[n-1] -= 2.0
    c2[n-1+i-j] += 1.0
    c2[n-1+j-i] += 1.0
    return c2, float(c2.max())

def apply_pair_flip(h,c,omhr,i,j,n):
   # same updates on c in place
   c[i:i+n] -= omhr
   c[n-1-i:n-1-i+n] += h
   c[j:j+n] += omhr
   c[n-1-j:n-1-j+n] -= h
   c[n-1] -= 2.0
   c[n-1+i-j] += 1.0
   c[n-1+j-i] += 1.0
   h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
   omhr[n-1-i]=1.0; omhr[n-1-j]=0.0
   return float(c.max())
```
Need old h/omhr for updates; if we update c in place and then h, okay because updates use old h/omhr except if h and omhr are views? omhr separate. Good. But if i and j order? We assume i is one, j zero. Ensure.

Random selection in binary optimize:
```
def rand_one(h,n,rng):
   while True:
      i=int(rng.randint(n))
      if h[i] >= 0.5: return i
```
Could infinite if no ones; not.

       i=rand_one; j=rand_zero
       if i==j: continue

           c=c2; cmax=nm; h[i]=... (or use apply? c2 already candidate; but h/omhr need update)
           # Since c2 from old, we can set h, omhr
           h[i]=0; h[j]=1; omhr[...]=...
           trials+=1; no=0
           if cmax < best_c: best_h=h.copy(); best_c=cmax; no_global=0
       else:
           no+=1; trials+=1
   # If phase improved, continue from current. If not, perturb current or from best?
   # Perturb from best to restart? Maybe from current if got stuck? Use best for fresh.
   # Make a perturbation of best_h: take best_h, flip k random pairs (accept regardless)
   h=best_h.copy(); c=correlate from h; cmax=...; omhr=...
   # random flips k (min pertur, n/20)
   for _ in range(k):
      i,j random; apply_pair_flip(...)
   # Then loop; track if best improved since perturb
   phases+=1
   # stop if phases too many and no global improvement
```
But if we always reset to best_h and perturb randomly, may repeat same perturbation. Use rng.
Set `max_phase_trials` maybe 20000. For 1100 budget, binary maybe 200s; n=500 20000 trials per phase, each ~0.1ms? maybe 2s per phase, can do many.

Let's write project_simplex robust:
```
def project_simplex(h,n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    target = 0.5*n
    h = np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    if abs(h.sum()-target) < 1e-13:
        return h
    lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
    # if target 0 or n? not; still
    for _ in range(60):
       mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
       s=np.clip(h+mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
       if s < target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    y=np.clip(h+hi,0.0,1.0)
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       mask=(y>1e-9)&(y<1.0-1e-9)
       cnt=mask.sum()
       if cnt>0:
          y[mask]+=diff/cnt
       else:
          # adjust element with max slack
          if diff>0:
             idx=int(np.argmax(1.0-y))
          else:
             idx=int(np.argmax(y))
          y[idx]=np.clip(y[idx]+diff,0.0,1.0)
       y=np.clip(y,0.0,1.0)
    # final tiny correction
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-14:
       mask=(y>1e-12)&(y<1.0-1e-12)
       if mask.any(): y[mask]+=diff/mask.sum()
       else:
          idx=int(np.argmax((y-1e-12)*(1.0-1e-12))) # maybe not
          y[idx]=np.clip(y[idx]+diff,0.0,1.0)
    return y
```
The `idx` argmax formula may be weird if diff negative. Could simpler: distribute diff over all non-bound; if no non-bound, choose argmax((1-abs(2y-1)))? But target feasible, blue. Use:
```
if diff>0: idx=int(np.argmax(1-y)) else idx=int(np.argmax(y))
```
Then clip; if correction not exactly due bound, residual tiny.
60 bisection enough.

### smooth_val_grad implementation
```
def smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    c=np.correlate(x,1.0-x,mode='full')
    m=float(c.max())
    r=alpha*(c-m)
    r = r - r.max()
    ee=np.exp(r)
    z=float(ee.sum())
    dx=2.0/n
    val=(m + np.log(z)/alpha)*dx
    w=ee/z
    omh=1.0-x
    A=np.convolve(omh,w,mode='full')
    C=np.convolve(x,w[::-1],mode='full')
    s=n-1
    grad=(A-C)[s:s+n]*dx
    return float(val), (grad if grad.ndim==1 else grad)
```
For alpha very small? alpha>0. Use alpha as float.
Could avoid `np.log(z)` if z=1? fine.

### penal functions
```
def penal_obj(x,n,alpha,pen):
    val,_=smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)
    s=x.sum()-0.5*n
    return val+0.5*pen*s*s
```
`penal_grad` similar.

### lbfgs_polish
```
def lbfgs_polish(h,n,alphas,pens,maxiter,deadline):
    h=project_simplex(h,n).copy()
    best_c=compute_c5(h,n); best_h=h.copy()
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    for al in alphas:
      if time.time()>deadline: break
      for pen in pens:
        if time.time()>deadline: break
        try:
           res=minimize(penal_obj,best_h,args=(n,al,pen),jac=penal_grad,method='L-BFGS-B',bounds=bounds,options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12})
        except Exception: continue
        nh=project_simplex(res.x,n); nc=compute_c5(nh,n)
        if nc < best_c - 1e-13:
           best_h=nh; best_c=nc
    return best_h,best_c
```
L-BFGS-B may take long with pen large. Use maxiter 80 maybe. We'll pass 100.

### coord_eval
```
def coord_eval(c,A,xs,old,n):
    d=xs-old
    cand = c[None,:] + d[:,None]*A[None,:]
    cand[:, n-1] -= d*d
    return cand.max(axis=1)
```
Allocating `c[None,:]` each. Fine. `xs` can include old; maxs maybe exactly cmax.

### coord_cd
We need be careful with modifying `c` and `h` while using `A` for each p. Since `A` uses `h` and `omhr`. After accepting, update h/omhr. Good.
But when we compute coarse maxs, we use `cur_max = float(c.max())`. If a candidate `d=0`, maxs = cmax? Due diag correction 0, yes. But if floating, okay. If no improvement, not accept.
After pass projection, if projection worsens, revert to best_h. But the `c`we computed after projection is for reverted? Let's code:
```
for pass:
   h_pass = h.copy()? To revert if projection worsens. We can store h0=h.copy() before pass.
   c = correlate(h)
   ...modify h/c...
   h_proj = project_simplex(h_pass,n)
   c_proj = correlate(h_proj)
   c_proj_val = max*dx
   if c_proj_val < best_c - tol:
       h = h_proj; c=c_proj; best_c=...
   else:
       # keep previous best h
       h = best_h? But we already have h variable best before pass.
       # We'll have best_h separate.
```
Initialize best_h copy. At start of each pass: `h_work = best_h.copy()`, `c = correlate(h_work)`. Modify. At end project and compare. If better, update best_h. Else keep previous. This is clean.
```
best_h = project(h).copy(); best_c=compute
for passes:
   h = best_h.copy(); c=correlate(h); omhr=...
   improved=False
   for p...
   h_proj=project(h)
   if compute(h_proj) < best_c:
       best_h=h_proj; best_c=...
   else:
       # maybe h before projection infeasible; no update
       if not improved: break
       # if improved but projection worsened, continue from best_h next pass
```
If no improvement during pass, break (feasible local with pass projection). Good.
This avoids reverting logic.

In pass, if we use `c.max()` each p, c is infeasible current after coordinate updates; okay. We don't update `best_c` until projection.

def binary_optimize(h_init,n,rng,deadline):
    b=binarize(h_init,n)
    if b is None: return None, np.inf
    dx=2.0/n

    stagnant=0
    max_phase_trials=50000
    plateau_limit=8000
    perturb_k=12
    while time.time() < deadline:
       # Start phase from best_h perturbed if stagnant>0? For first phase use h.
       if h is None? 
       # If current h equals best? We'll perturb each phase.
       h_work=best_h.copy()
       # If first phase no perturbation? We can perturb even first? initial binarized maybe already; perturb can explore.
       if rng.randint(100) < 10: # do not perturb first? 
           pass
       for _ in range(perturb_k):

       plateau=0; trials=0

       # if no global improvement after 3-4 phases, break

def rand_one_zero(h,n,rng):
    while True:
       i=int(rng.randint(n))
       if h[i] >= 0.5: break
    while True:
       j=int(rng.randint(n))
       if h[j] < 0.5: break
       if j==i: pass? 
    return i,j
```

### Transfer and coarser evaluation

def transfer_h(h,n,n_tgt):
   if n_tgt == n: return project_simplex(h,n_tgt).copy()
   xs=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n)
   yt=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_tgt)
   return project_simplex(np.interp(yt,xs,h), n_tgt)
```

### Let's refine binary_optimize to avoid many branches
```
def binary_optimize(h_init,n,rng,deadline):
    b=binarize(h_init,n)
    if b is None: return None, np.inf
    dx=2.0/n
    best=project_simplex(b,n).copy()
    bc=np.correlate(best,1-best,'full'); best_cmax=float(bc.max())
    # use first state
    first=True
    stagnant=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
       if first:
          h=best.copy(); c=bc.copy(); first=False
       else:
          h=best.copy()
          # perturb 8 random flips
          for _ in range(8):
             i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng)
             if i!=j:
                c临时? We need c updated; get c from correlate initial
          c=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')
       cmax=float(c.max()); omhr=1.0-h[::-1]
       plateau=0; trials=0; phase_improve=False
       while time.time()<deadline and plateau<6000 and trials<40000:
          i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng)
          c2,nm=binary_trial(c,h,omhr,i,j,n)
          trials+=1
          if nm < cmax - 1e-9:
             c=c2; cmax=nm
             h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
             omhr[n-1-i]=1.0; omhr[n-1-j]=0.0
             plateau=0; phase_improve=True
             if cmax < best_cmax:
                best=h.copy(); best_cmax=cmax
          else:
             plateau+=1
       if phase_improve and cmax < best_cmax: best... (already)
       stagnant = 0 if phase_improve? But if improved current but not best? Use best improvement.
       if cmax < best_cmax:
          best... stagnant=0
       else:
          stagnant +=1
          if stagnant > 3: break
    return best, best_cmax*dx
```
If first state no perturbation. Perturb count 8. If no improvement after 3 perturb phases, stop.
Potential issue: In else branch, c not updated before loop if perturb. We'll compute c after perturb.
Need if `rand_one_zero` called on h after perturb where sum half, okay.
Perturb flips may make c worse; c recomputed.

But in first state, `bc` is correlate of b. Good.
When best changes, next phases perturb from best.

This code okay.

### Coordinate CD line count reduction
In coord_cd, candidate refinement code maybe long. We'll write compact loops.
```
def coord_cd(h,n,max_passes=4,deadline=None,grid=32,refine=10,tol=1e-12):
    best=project_simplex(h,n).copy(); best_c=compute_c5(best,n)
    dx=2.0/n; L=2*n-1; diag=n-1; A=np.zeros(L)
    for _ in range(max_passes):
       if deadline and time.time()>deadline: break
       h=best.copy(); c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,'full'); omhr=1.0-h[::-1]
       improved=False
       for p in range(n):
           old=h[p]
           A.fill(0.0); A[p:p+n]+=omhr; st=diag-p; A[st:st+n]-=h
           cur=float(c.max())
           xs=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,grid)
           mvals=coord_eval(c,A,xs,old,n)
           bi=int(np.argmin(mvals)); bx=float(xs[bi]); bv=float(mvals[bi])
           if bv < cur - tol:
              step=1.0/(grid-1)
              lo=max(0.0,bx-step); hi=min(1.0,bx+step)
              xx=np.linspace(lo,hi,refine+1)
              mm=coord_eval(c,A,xx,old,n)
              ii=int(np.argmin(mm))
              if mm[ii] < bv - tol: bx=float(xx[ii]); bv=float(mm[ii])
              if lo < bx < hi:
                 step2=(hi-lo)/max(1,refine)
                 lo2=max(0.0,bx-step2); hi2=min(1.0,bx+step2)
                 yy=np.linspace(lo2,hi2,9)
                 mo=coord_eval(c,A,yy,old,n)
                 io=int(np.argmin(mo))
                 if mo[io] < bv - tol: bx=float(yy[io]); bv=float(mo[io])
              if bv < cur - tol:
                 d=bx-old
                 c[p:p+n]+=d*omhr; c[st:st+n]-=d*h; c[diag]-=d*d
                 h[p]=bx; omhr[diag-(p)? n-1-p] = 1-bx
                 improved=True
       h2=project_simplex(h,n)
       c2v=compute_c5(h2,n)
       if c2v < best_c - tol:
          best=h2; best_c=c2v
       if not improved: break
    return best,best_c
```

def binary_optimize(h_init,n,rng,deadline):

Now binary_optimize code:
```
def binary_optimize(h_init,n,rng,deadline):
    b=binarize(h_init,n)
    if b is None: return None, np.inf
    dx=2.0/n
    best=project_simplex(b,n).copy()
    bc=np.correlate(best,1.0-best,'full'); best_cmax=float(bc.max())
    first=True; stagnant=0
    while time.time()<deadline:
       if first:
          h=best.copy(); c=bc.copy(); first=False
       else:
          h=best.copy()
          for _ in range(6):
             i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng)
             if i != j:
                h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
          c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,'full')
       cmax=float(c.max()); omhr=1.0-h[::-1]
       plateau=0; phase_trials=0; improved_phase=False
       while time.time()<deadline and plateau<5000 and phase_trials<30000:
          i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng)
          if i==j:
             continue
          c2,nm=binary_trial(c,h,omhr,i,j,n)
          phase_trials +=1
          if nm < cmax - 1e-9:
             c=c2; cmax=nm
             h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
             omhr[n-1-i]=1.0; omhr[n-1-j]=0.0
             plateau=0; improved_phase=True
             if cmax < best_cmax:
                best=h.copy(); best_cmax=cmax; stagnant=0
          else:
             plateau +=1
       if phase_trials > 0 and cmax < best_cmax:
          best=h.copy(); best_cmax=cmax; stagnant=0
       else:
          stagnant +=1
          if stagnant > 3:
             break
    return best, best_cmax*dx
```
If first state local improves, subsequent phases perturb from best. If cmax is already lower than best? best_cmax updated within loop. At end updated too. Good.
If no improvement for 3 perturb phases, stop. Could stop too early if improvements require many perturb; but budget enough for other methods.

Potential issue: `c2` is a new array; after many accepts c becomes c2. `omhr` updated. Good.

`rand_one_zero`: If h has no ones/zeros due bug, infinite. Add a safety count:
```
def rand_one_zero(h,n,rng):
    for _ in range(100):
       i=int(rng.randint(n))
       if h[i] >= 0.5: break
    else:
       i=int(np.argmax(h))
    for _ in range(100):
       j=int(rng.randint(n))
       if h[j] < 0.5: break
    else:
       j=int(np.argmin(h))
    if i==j:
       # fallback
       ones=np.flatnonzero(h>=0.5); zeros=np.flatnonzero(h<0.5)
       i=int(ones[0]); j=int(zeros[0])
    return i,j
```
But `np.flatnonzero` in rare fallback. Good.

def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    deadline=t0 + max(5, budget_s - 5)
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)

    def? no.
    # sym variants
    rev=h0[::-1]
    for v in (0.5*(h0+rev), 0.5*(h0+(1.0-rev))):
       vv=project_simplex(v,n0); cv=compute_c5(vv,n0)
       if cv < best_c: best...
    if time.time() > deadline - 30:
       final=project; return

       if c1 < best_c: ...

       if c1 < best_c: ...
       h1,c1=coord_cd(h1,n_best, max_passes=5, deadline=t_cont, grid=28, refine=10)
       if c1 < best_c: ...
    # binary n_best

       hb,cb=binary_optimize(best_h,n_best,rng,t_bin)

          if ...
          h1,c1=coord_cd(... max_passes=3, deadline=t_bin2,grid=32)
          update
    # other n
    n_opts = []
    base = n_best
    for cand in [base-100, base-50, base+50, base+100, 400, 500, 600]:
       if cand>=100 and cand<=1000 and cand%2==0 and cand not in n_opts: n_opts.append(cand)
    # maybe include 300/250 if base large? Could lower. But keep <1k.

       ht=transfer_h(best_h,n_best,nt); cv=compute_c5(ht,nt)
       if cv < best_c:
          best_h=ht; best_c=cv; n_best=nt
       # small optimize if time

    # final polish best

       update

    final_h=project_simplex(best_h,n_best); final_c=compute_c5(final_h,n_best)

### `coord_cd` vectorized memory and speed
`coord_eval` for K ~ 36, L ~ 1000. It creates `d[:,None]*A[None,:]` (K,L), then adds c. Then subtract diag. Good.
But `A` filled with `omhr` and `h` which are length n. `A[st:st+n] -= h`. Slices overlap? If p and st ranges overlap (always around diag), addition order: first add omhr, then subtract h. The linear coefficient for diag should `omhr[n-1-p] - h[p] = (1-h[p]) - h[p] = 1-2h[p]`. Since slices overlap only at diag? For p? The two slices [p,p+n] and [n-1-p,n-1-p+n] overlap length: end_high = p+n-1, start_low=n-1-p. Overlap if p+n-1 >= n-1-p -> 2p >=0 yes; length = p? Actually overlap from n-1-p to p? length 2p? Wait n=500,p=100: first [100,599], second [399,898], overlap [399,599] length201. Yes, p can be first and second for many lags? Earlier we thought for a fixed lag p can be first or second, but for a given lag m, p could be both if τ=0 only? How then coefficient in overlapping region is both? Let's examine: For p, as first range m=p..n-1+p, as second range m=n-1-p..n-1+p. They overlap for m between n-1-p and p if p close to n/2. For such m, does h_p participate both as first and second in the same lag? A lag τ=m-(n-1). p as first if τ=p-i for some i, p as second if i=p. Both conditions in same lag if there exists i such that i+τ=p and i=p -> τ=0? But the ranges overlap for p=100, m=500 (n=500 => diag499? slight). Let's test p=100,n=500, m=500, τ=1. p as first: i=p-τ=99 valid. p as second: i=p=100, first index=101. These are two different pairs in the same lag m: one pair (first p, second 99), another pair (first 101, second p). Both include h_p, so coefficient includes contributions from two distinct terms! Ah earlier I thought at most one, but for a fixed lag, p can appear in multiple pairs if the lag window includes p at both positions in different terms. Indeed c_m = sum over i=0..N-1 h_{i+τ}(1-h_i). For fixed τ, h_p can be first in term i=p-τ, and second in term i=p, if both i valid. So coefficient can have two contributions for same lag. Our slice addition method correctly adds both. Good. So A overlaps with sum of contributions. Our earlier coefficient intervals were not exclusive. Great. The exact linear update with two slices works for all terms.
Self diag: if p as first and second in same term? Only when τ=0 and same i=p. The slices include at m=diag: first contributes from i=p, second contributes from i=p; total 1-2h_p. Plus diagonal quadratic -d^2. Our A + diag correction correct. Good.

Thus coordinate update exact.

### Binary trial update with two slices similarly accounts for multiple pair contributions. Cross corrections: For two variables i,j, in a given lag, they could both appear in two separate pairs (i first with j? and j first with i?) The cross correction -d_a d_b for each ordered pair (a,b) that appears as a pair (first a, second b). Our two point corrections cover ordered pairs if |i-j| valid. But what if i and j both appear in same lag but not as paired to each other, e.g., p appears in two terms with q and r; for two variables i,j, they could both be first in two terms with different second, not a product d_i*d_j? The quadratic cross term in expansion only arises for terms where both variables appear in the same product h_a(1-h_b), i.e., a and b are the two variables in that term. So ordered pairs. Thus point corrections correct.

But if the two variables are the same? no. If i/j difference s, ordered pair (i,j) at lag i-j, and (j,i) at lag j-i. What about both as first with different second variables? No cross between d_i and d_j because term involves only one changed variable; linear handles. Good.

### Binary exact update test with a case where i,j both in same lag but not paired? For lag τ=1, pairs (0,1),(1,2),(2,3). If i=0,j=1, they are paired, cross. If i=0,j=2, in same lag τ=2? pairs (0,2) cross. In lag τ=1, i=0 first with 1, j=2 second with1 or first with? The terms: i=0 as first with 1 (linear), j=2 as second with1 (linear); no product between i and j. Good.

Thus binary updates exact.

Full code:
```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

def project_simplex(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    if abs(h.sum() - target) < 1e-13:
        return h
    lo, hi = -1.0, 1.0
    for _ in range(60):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        if np.clip(h + mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum() < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    y = np.clip(h + hi, 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - y.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        mask = (y > 1e-9) & (y < 1.0 - 1e-9)
        cnt = int(mask.sum())
        if cnt:
            y[mask] += diff / cnt
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - y)) if diff > 0 else int(np.argmax(y))
            y[idx] = np.clip(y[idx] + diff, 0.0, 1.0)
        y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - y.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-14:
        mask = (y > 1e-12) & (y < 1.0 - 1e-12)
        if mask.any():
            y[mask] += diff / mask.sum()
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - y)) if diff > 0 else int(np.argmax(y))
            y[idx] = np.clip(y[idx] + diff, 0.0, 1.0)
    return y
```
Potential in fallback `idx` if all y=0 and diff>0, argmax(1-y) arbitrary; adding diff may exceed1? If target infeasible impossible. okay.

```
def compute_c5(h,n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')
    return float(c.max()) * (2.0/n)
```

Smooth:
```
def smooth_val_grad(x, n, alpha):
    c = np.correlate(x, 1.0 - x, mode='full')
    m = float(c.max())
    r = alpha * (c - m)
    r = r - r.max()
    ee = np.exp(r)
    z = float(ee.sum())
    dx = 2.0 / n
    val = (m + np.log(z) / alpha) * dx
    w = ee / z
    A = np.convolve(1.0 - x, w, mode='full')
    C = np.convolve(x, w[::-1], mode='full')
    s = n - 1
    grad = (A - C)[s:s+n] * dx
    return float(val), np.asarray(grad, dtype=float)
```
If alpha is int and z maybe? okay.

```
def smooth_obj(x,n,alpha): return smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)[0]
def smooth_grad(x,n,alpha): return smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)[1]
def sum_con(x,n): return x.sum() - 0.5*n
def sum_con_jac(x,n): return np.ones_like(x, dtype=float)
def penal_obj(x,n,alpha,pen):
    val,_ = smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)
    s = x.sum() - 0.5*n
    return val + 0.5*pen*s*s
def penal_grad(x,n,alpha,pen):
    _,g = smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha)
    s = x.sum() - 0.5*n
    return g + (pen*s)*np.ones_like(g, dtype=float)
```

Polish functions:
```
def lbfgs_polish(h,n,alphas,pens,maxiter,deadline):
    h = project_simplex(h,n).astype(float, copy=True)
    best = h.copy(); best_c = compute_c5(best,n)
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)] * n
    for al in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        for pe in pens:
            if time.time() > deadline: break
            try:
                res = minimize(penal_obj, best, args=(n,al,pe), jac=penal_grad, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12})
            except Exception:
                continue
            nh = project_simplex(res.x,n)
            nc = compute_c5(nh,n)
            if nc < best_c - 1e-13:
                best = nh; best_c = nc
    return best,best_c
```
SLSQP similar.

```
def slsqp_polish(h,n,alphas,maxiter,deadline):
    best=project_simplex(h,n).copy(); best_c=compute_c5(best,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum_con,'jac':sum_con_jac,'args':(n,)}]
    for al in alphas:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       try:
          res=minimize(smooth_obj,best,args=(n,al),jac=smooth_grad,method='SLSQP',constraints=cons,bounds=bounds,options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12})
       except Exception:
          continue
       nh=project_simplex(res.x,n); nc=compute_c5(nh,n)
       if nc<best_c-1e-13:
          best=nh; best_c=nc
    return best,best_c
```
Good.

Coord:
```
def coord_eval(c,A,xs,old,n):
    d = xs - old
    cand = c[None, :] + d[:, None] * A[None, :]
    cand[:, n-1] -= d*d
    return cand.max(axis=1)
```
Potential if xs is list, `d` array. Good.

```
def coord_cd(h,n,max_passes=4,deadline=None,grid=32,refine=10,tol=1e-12):
    best = project_simplex(h,n).astype(float, copy=True)
    best_c = compute_c5(best,n)
    dx = 2.0/n
    L = 2*n -1
    diag = n-1
    A = np.zeros(L)
    xs0 = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,grid)
    for _ in range(max_passes):
       if deadline is not None and time.time() > deadline: break
       h = best.copy()
       c = np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
       omhr = 1.0 - h[::-1]
       improved = False
       for p in range(n):
           old = float(h[p])
           A.fill(0.0)
           A[p:p+n] += omhr
           st = diag - p
           A[st:st+n] -= h
           cur = float(c.max())
           mvals = coord_eval(c,A,xs0,old,n)
           bi = int(np.argmin(mvals))
           bx = float(xs0[bi]); bv = float(mvals[bi])
           if bv < cur - tol:
              step = 1.0/(grid-1) if grid > 1 else 1.0
              lo = max(0.0, bx-step)
              hi = min(1.0, bx+step)
              xx = np.linspace(lo,hi,refine+1)
              mm = coord_eval(c,A,xx,old,n)
              ii = int(np.argmin(mm))
              if mm[ii] < bv - tol:
                 bx = float(xx[ii]); bv = float(mm[ii])
              if lo < bx < hi:
                 step2 = (hi-lo)/float(refine) if refine>0 else 0.0
                 lo2 = max(0.0, bx-step2)
                 hi2 = min(1.0, bx+step2)
                 yy = np.linspace(lo2,hi2,9)
                 mo = coord_eval(c,A,yy,old,n)
                 io = int(np.argmin(mo))
                 if mo[io] < bv - tol:
                    bx = float(yy[io]); bv = float(mo[io])
              if bv < cur - tol:
                 d = bx - old
                 c[p:p+n] += d*omhr
                 c[st:st+n] -= d*h
                 c[diag] -= d*d
                 h[p] = bx
                 omhr[diag-p] = 1.0 - bx
                 improved = True
       hp = project_simplex(h,n)
       cp = compute_c5(hp,n)
       if cp < best_c - tol:
          best = hp; best_c = cp
       if not improved:
          break
    return best,best_c
```
Important: `omhr[diag-p]`? diag-p = n-1-p. Yes. But if `p` appears, omhr index maps reversed h. Good.
Potential bug: `diagonal` coordinate p for p: `st=diag-p`; slice start can be negative? If p > diag? p max n-1, diag-p min0. Good. End st+n = diag-p+n = n-1-p+n = 2n-1-p <=2n-1. Good.
`A[p:p+n]` p max n-1, end 2n-1, length? If p=n-1, slice [n-1:2n-1] length n? End exclusive 2n-1, length n? indices n-1..2n-2 count n. Good.

In refinement, `if lo < bx < hi`: hi exclusive; if best at endpoint, no second refine. Good.

Potential issue: `c.max()` each p O(L). Could be okay. But after we update c, the current max maybe not equal `float(c.max())` for next p computed anew.

Binary:
```
def binarize(h,n):
    if n % 2: return None
    target = n//2
    if target <=0 or target >= n:
       b = np.zeros(n,float); b[:target]=1; return b
    idx = np.argpartition(h, target-1)[:target]
    b = np.zeros(n,float); b[idx]=1.0
    return b
```
If target=0? n=0 not. fine.

`rand_one_zero`:
```
def rand_one_zero(h,n,rng):
    i = -1
    for _ in range(60):
       i = int(rng.randint(n))
       if h[i] >= 0.5: break
    if i < 0: i = int(np.argmax(h))
    j = -1
    for _ in range(60):
       j = int(rng.randint(n))
       if h[j] < 0.5: break
    if j < 0: j = int(np.argmin(h))
    if i == j:
       ones = np.flatnonzero(h >= 0.5)
       zeros = np.flatnonzero(h < 0.5)
       if ones.size and zeros.size:
          i = int(ones[0]); j = int(zeros[0])
       else:
          j = -1
    if i < 0 or j < 0: return -1,-1
    return i,j
```
If returns -1, caller handle.

`binary_trial`:
```
def binary_trial(c,h,omhr,i,j,n):
    d = c.copy()
    d[i:i+n] -= omhr
    st = n-1-i
    d[st:st+n] += h
    d[j:j+n] += omhr
    st = n-1-j
    d[st:st+n] -= h
    d[n-1] -= 2.0
    d[n-1+i-j] += 1.0
    d[n-1+j-i] += 1.0
    return d, float(d.max())
```
If n=1? Not; n>1. Indices valid. For i/j maybe -1? handle before.

`binary_optimize` as above. Need `dx = 2.0/n`.
In perturb, `rand_one_zero` can return -1. Check.

```
def binary_optimize(h_init,n,rng,deadline):
    b = binarize(h_init,n)
    if b is None: return None, np.inf
    dx=2.0/n
    best=project_simplex(b,n).copy()
    bc=np.correlate(best,1.0-best,mode='full')
    best_cmax=float(bc.max())
    first=True; stagnant=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
       if first:
          h=best.copy(); c=bc.copy(); first=False
       else:
          h=best.copy()
          for _ in range(6):
             i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng)
             if i>=0 and j>=0 and i!=j:
                h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
          c=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
       cmax=float(c.max()); omhr=1.0-h[::-1]
       plateau=0; trials=0; improved=False
       while time.time() < deadline and plateau < 6000 and trials < 40000:
          i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng)
          if i<0 or j<0 or i==j: continue
          c2,nm=binary_trial(c,h,omhr,i,j,n)
          trials += 1
          if nm < cmax - 1e-9:
             c=c2; cmax=nm
             h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
             omhr[n-1-i]=1.0; omhr[n-1-j]=0.0
             plateau=0; improved=True
             if cmax < best_cmax:
                best=h.copy(); best_cmax=cmax; stagnant=0
          else:
             plateau += 1
       if cmax < best_cmax:
          best=h.copy(); best_cmax=cmax; stagnant=0
       else:
          stagnant += 1
          if stagnant > 3: break
    return best, best_cmax*dx
```
If first phase no improvement, stagnant=1, perturb. Good.
Potential issue: After a phase, if cmax improved but we updated best inside, then at end cmax < best_cmax false (equal), so stagnant +=1 even though improved current? Actually inside if `cmax < best_cmax` when new lower than previous, sets best_cmax=cmax. At end `if cmax < best_cmax` false (equal), else stagnant++ despite phase improved current to best. We should track `old_best` or `phase_best_improve`. Use `phase_best = improved`? Better:
```
phase_best_improve = False
inside:
   if cmax < best_cmax:
       best=...; best_cmax=cmax; stagnant=0; phase_best_improve=True
end:
   if phase_best_improve:
       pass
   else:
       stagnant +=1; if stagnant>3: break
```
If phase improves current to new best, we don't want stagnant. If phase improved but not best, maybe stagnant? Could still be useful but perturb. Use `if not phase_best_improve: stagnant +=1`. Good.
Add variable.

Also if first state improves best inside, stagnant=0. Good.

### Transfer function
```
def transfer_h(h,n,n_tgt):
    if n_tgt == n: return project_simplex(h,n_tgt).copy()
    x = np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n)
    y = np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_tgt)
    return project_simplex(np.interp(y,x,h), n_tgt)
```

### Run detailed
Need handle `budget_s` maybe float. Use `budget_s = float(budget_s)`. Deadlines:
```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time(); budget_s=float(budget_s); deadline=t0+max(5.0,budget_s-5.0)
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
       h0=np.array(initial_h_values,dtype=float).copy(); n0=int(len(h0))
    except Exception:
       n0=400; h0=np.full(n0,0.5)
    h0=project_simplex(h0,n0)
    best_h=h0.copy(); best_c=float(compute_c5(h0,n0)); n_best=n0
    if n_best % 2 == 0:
       hb=binarize(h0,n_best)
       if hb is not None:
          cb=compute_c5(hb,n_best)
          if cb < best_c: best_h=hb; best_c=cb
    rev=h0[::-1]
    for vv in (0.5*(h0+rev), 0.5*(h0+(1.0-rev))):
       vv=project_simplex(vv,n_best); cv=compute_c5(vv,n_best)
       if cv < best_c: best_h=vv; best_c=cv
    if time.time() > deadline - 30:
       return project_simplex(best_h,n_best), float(best_c), int(n_best)
    t_cont = min(deadline - 250.0, t0 + max(40.0, 0.35*budget_s))
    if time.time() < t_cont:
       h1,c1=lbfgs_polish(best_h,n_best,[100,1000,10000],[1e3,1e4],80,t_cont)
       if c1 < best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1
       h1,c1=slsqp_polish(best_h,n_best,[2000,20000,200000,2000000],250,t_cont)
       if c1 < best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1
       h1,c1=coord_cd(best_h,n_best,max_passes=5,deadline=t_cont,grid=28,refine=10)
       if c1 < best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1
    t_bin = min(deadline - 150.0, t0 + max(80.0, 0.60*budget_s))
    if n_best % 2 == 0 and time.time() < t_bin:
       hb,cb=binary_optimize(best_h,n_best,rng,t_bin)
       if cb < best_c:
          best_h=hb; best_c=cb
          t_extra = min(deadline - 120.0, time.time() + 90.0)
          h1,c1=slsqp_polish(best_h,n_best,[20000,200000,2000000],120,t_extra)
          if c1 < best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1
          h1,c1=coord_cd(best_h,n_best,max_passes=3,deadline=t_extra,grid=32,refine=12)
          if c1 < best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1
    # variant n
    cands = [400,300,600,500,450,550,350,700]
    tried = [n_best]
    for nt in cands:
       if len(tried) >= 5: break
       if time.time() > deadline - 90: break
       if nt in tried or nt < 100 or nt > 1000 or nt % 2: continue
       tried.append(nt)
       ht = transfer_h(best_h, n_best, nt)
       cv = compute_c5(ht, nt)
       if cv < best_c:
          best_h = ht; best_c = cv; n_best = nt
       chunk = min(deadline - 70.0, time.time() + 80.0)
       if time.time() < chunk:
          h1,c1=slsqp_polish(ht,nt,[20000],60,chunk)
          h1,c1=coord_cd(h1,nt,max_passes=2,deadline=chunk,grid=24,refine=8)
          if c1 < best_c:
             best_h=h1; best_c=c1; n_best=nt
          if nt % 2 == 0:
             hb,cb=binary_optimize(ht,nt,rng,chunk)
             if cb < best_c:
                best_h=hb; best_c=cb; n_best=nt
    # final polish
    tfin1 = min(deadline - 30.0, time.time() + 80.0)
    if time.time() < tfin1:
       h1,c1=slsqp_polish(best_h,n_best,[100000,1000000],200,tfin1)
       if c1 < best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1
    tfin2 = min(deadline - 5.0, time.time() + 120.0)
    if time.time() < tfin2:
       h1,c1=coord_cd(best_h,n_best,max_passes=3,deadline=tfin2,grid=40,refine=14)
       if c1 < best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1
    if n_best % 2 == 0 and time.time() < deadline - 10:
       hb,cb=binary_optimize(best_h,n_best,rng,deadline)
       if cb < best_c: best_h=hb; best_c=cb
    final_h = project_simplex(best_h, n_best)
    final_c = float(compute_c5(final_h, n_best))
    return final_h, final_c, int(n_best)
```

def rand_one_zero(h,n,rng):
    i = int(rng.randint(n))
    while h[i] < 0.5: i = int(rng.randint(n))
    j = int(rng.randint(n))
    while h[j] >= 0.5: j = int(rng.randint(n))
    return i,j
```

Compact project:
```
def project_simplex(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,float).clip(0,1); t=0.5*n
    if abs(h.sum()-t)<1e-13: return h
    lo,hi=-1.0,1.0
    for _ in range(50):
       m=0.5*(lo+hi)
       if np.clip(h+m,0,1).sum()<t: lo=m
       else: hi=m
    y=np.clip(h+hi,0,1); d=t-y.sum()
    if abs(d)>1e-12:
       ms=(y>1e-9)&(y<1-1e-9)
       if ms.any(): y[ms]+=d/ms.sum()
       else: y[int(np.argmax(1-y) if d>0 else np.argmax(y))]+=d
    return np.clip(y,0,1)
```
Need np.int? `int(np.argmax(...))`. Good. Residual after clip tiny. Maybe one more correction not needed. This shorter. But if no mask and adding d exceeds bound, clip may make sum not exact. But rare. Could add final average correction:
```
d=t-y.sum(); if abs(d)>1e-14: ...
```
Keep two stages? To be safe, use 50 bisect + distribute; sum should be within 1e-12. One more tiny adjust:
```
d=t-y.sum(); ms=(y>1e-12)&(y<1-1e-12);
if d and ms.any(): y[ms]+=d/ms.sum()
```
Good. Compact.

Smooth compact:
```
def smooth_val_grad(x,n,alpha):
    c=np.correlate(x,1-x,'full'); m=c.max(); r=alpha*(c-m); r-=r.max()
    ee=np.exp(r); z=ee.sum(); dx=2/n
    w=ee/z
    A=np.convolve(1-x,w,'full'); C=np.convolve(x,w[::-1],'full')
    s=n-1
    return (m+np.log(z)/alpha)*dx, (A-C)[s:s+n]*dx
```
Then `smooth_obj`, `smooth_grad`.

SLSQP compact:
```
def slsqp_polish(h,n,alphas,maxiter,deadline):
    h=project_simplex(h,n); best=h.copy(); bc=compute_c5(h,n); bounds=[(0.,1.)]*n
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum_con,'jac':sum_con_jac,'args':(n,)}]
    for a in alphas:
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       try:
          r=minimize(smooth_obj,best,args=(n,a),jac=smooth_grad,method='SLSQP',bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-12})
       except Exception: continue
       nh=project_simplex(r.x,n); nc=compute_c5(nh,n)
       if nc<bc: best=nh; bc=nc
    return best,bc
```
Good.

coord_cd compact: remove some comments.
```
def coord_cd(...):
    best=project_simplex(h,n); bc=compute_c5(best,n); L=2*n-1; ddiag=n-1; A=np.zeros(L); xs=np.linspace(0,1,grid)
    for _ in range(max_passes):
       if deadline and time.time()>deadline: break
       x=best.copy(); c=np.correlate(x,1-x,'full'); om=1-x[::-1]; imp=False
       for p in range(n):
          old=x[p]; A.fill(0.0); A[p:p+n]+=om; st=ddiag-p; A[st:st+n]-=x
          cur=c.max(); vals=coord_eval(c,A,xs,old,n)
          i=int(np.argmin(vals)); bx=xs[i]; bv=vals[i]
          if bv < cur - tol:
             sp=1.0/(grid-1); lo=max(0,bx-sp); hi=min(1,bx+sp)
             xx=np.linspace(lo,hi,refine+1); vv=coord_eval(c,A,xx,old,n); j=int(np.argmin(vv))
             if vv[j]<bv-tol: bx=xx[j]; bv=vv[j]
             if lo<bx<hi:
                sp2=(hi-lo)/refine; lo2=max(0,bx-sp2); hi2=min(1,bx+sp2)
                yy=np.linspace(lo2,hi2,9); ww=coord_eval(c,A,yy,old,n); k=int(np.argmin(ww))
                if ww[k]<bv-tol: bx=yy[k]; bv=ww[k]
             if bv < cur - tol:
                d=bx-old; c[p:p+n]+=d*om; c[st:st+n]-=d*x; c[ddiag]-=d*d; x[p]=bx; om[ddiag-p]=1-bx; imp=True
       xp=project_simplex(x,n); cp=compute_c5(xp,n)
       if cp<bc: best=xp; bc=cp
       if not imp: break
    return best,bc
```
Need `cur=float(c.max())`? c.max returns np float, comparisons okay. `grid-1` >0.

Binary_optimize compact:
```
def binary_optimize(h_init,n,rng,deadline):
    b=binarize(h_init,n)
    if b is None: return None, np.inf
    dx=2.0/n; best=project_simplex(b,n); bc=np.correlate(best,1-best,'full'); bcm=float(bc.max())
    first=True; stat=0
    while time.time()<deadline:
       if first: h=best.copy(); c=bc.copy(); first=False
       else:
          h=best.copy()
          for _ in range(6):
             i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng); h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
          c=np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')
       cm=float(c.max()); om=1-h[::-1]; plat=0; tr=0; pbest=False
       while time.time()<deadline and plat<6000 and tr<40000:
          i,j=rand_one_zero(h,n,rng)
          if i==j: continue
          c2,nm=binary_trial(c,h,om,i,j,n); tr+=1
          if nm < cm - 1e-9:
             c=c2; cm=nm; h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0; om[n-1-i]=1.0; om[n-1-j]=0.0; plat=0
             if cm < bcm:
                best=h.copy(); bcm=cm; pbest=True
          else: plat+=1
       if pbest: stat=0
       else:
          stat+=1
          if stat>3: break
    return best,bcm*dx
```
Potential: If phase improves best only at end but no inside? If cmax lower than bcm at start? Then each improvement resets. Good. If phase ends at cmax lower but no improvement? Phase always updates best inside if cm<bcm, so pbest true. If phase did not improve best but local state improved not below global, stat++. Good.

    return i,j
```

```python
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time


def project_simplex(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).clip(0.0, 1.0)
    t = 0.5 * n
    if abs(h.sum() - t) < 1e-13:
        return h
    lo, hi = -1.0, 1.0
    for _ in range(55):
        m = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        if np.clip(h + m, 0.0, 1.0).sum() < t:
            lo = m
        else:
            hi = m
    y = np.clip(h + hi, 0.0, 1.0)
    d = t - y.sum()
    if abs(d) > 1e-12:
        mask = (y > 1e-9) & (y < 1.0 - 1e-9)
        if mask.any():
            y[mask] += d / mask.sum()
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - y)) if d > 0 else int(np.argmax(y))
            y[idx] = np.clip(y[idx] + d, 0.0, 1.0)
    y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
    d = t - y.sum()
    if abs(d) > 1e-14:
        mask = (y > 1e-12) & (y < 1.0 - 1e-12)
        if mask.any():
            y[mask] += d / mask.sum()
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - y)) if d > 0 else int(np.argmax(y))
            y[idx] = np.clip(y[idx] + d, 0.0, 1.0)
    return y


def compute_c5(h, n):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(c.max()) * (2.0 / n)


def smooth_val_grad(x, n, alpha):
    c = np.correlate(x, 1.0 - x, mode="full")
    m = float(c.max())
    r = alpha * (c - m)
    r -= r.max()
    ee = np.exp(r)
    z = float(ee.sum())
    dx = 2.0 / n
    w = ee / z
    A = np.convolve(1.0 - x, w, mode="full")
    B = np.convolve(x, w[::-1], mode="full")
    s = n - 1
    return (m + np.log(z) / alpha) * dx, (A - B)[s:s + n] * dx


def smooth_obj(x, n, alpha):
    return smooth_val_grad(x, n, alpha)[0]


def smooth_grad(x, n, alpha):
    return smooth_val_grad(x, n, alpha)[1]


def sum_con(x, n):
    return x.sum() - 0.5 * n


def sum_con_jac(x, n):
    return np.ones_like(x, dtype=float)


def slsqp_polish(h, n, alphas, maxiter, deadline):
    h = project_simplex(h, n)
    best = h.copy()
    bc = compute_c5(best, n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    cons = [{"type": "eq", "fun": sum_con, "jac": sum_con_jac, "args": (n,)}]
    for a in alphas:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        try:
            res = minimize(
                smooth_obj, best, args=(n, a), jac=smooth_grad,
                method="SLSQP", bounds=bounds, constraints=cons,
                options={"maxiter": int(maxiter), "ftol": 1e-12}
            )
        except Exception:
            continue
        nh = project_simplex(res.x, n)
        nc = compute_c5(nh, n)
        if nc < bc - 1e-13:
            best = nh
            bc = nc
    return best, bc


def coord_eval(c, A, xs, old, n):
    d = xs - old
    cand = c[None, :] + d[:, None] * A[None, :]
    cand[:, n - 1] -= d * d
    return cand.max(axis=1)


def coord_cd(h, n, max_passes=4, deadline=None, grid=32, refine=10, tol=1e-12):
    best = project_simplex(h, n)
    bc = compute_c5(best, n)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    diag = n - 1
    A = np.zeros(L)
    xs0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, grid)
    for _ in range(max_passes):
        if deadline is not None and time.time() > deadline:
            break
        x = best.copy()
        c = np.correlate(x, 1.0 - x, mode="full")
        om = 1.0 - x[::-1]
        improved = False
        for p in range(n):
            old = float(x[p])
            A.fill(0.0)
            A[p:p + n] += om
            st = diag - p
            A[st:st + n] -= x
            cur = float(c.max())
            vals = coord_eval(c, A, xs0, old, n)
            i = int(np.argmin(vals))
            bx = float(xs0[i])
            bv = float(vals[i])
            if bv < cur - tol:
                sp = 1.0 / (grid - 1) if grid > 1 else 1.0
                lo = max(0.0, bx - sp)
                hi = min(1.0, bx + sp)
                xx = np.linspace(lo, hi, refine + 1)
                mm = coord_eval(c, A, xx, old, n)
                j = int(np.argmin(mm))
                if mm[j] < bv - tol:
                    bx = float(xx[j])
                    bv = float(mm[j])
                if lo < bx < hi:
                    sp2 = (hi - lo) / float(refine)
                    lo2 = max(0.0, bx - sp2)
                    hi2 = min(1.0, bx + sp2)
                    yy = np.linspace(lo2, hi2, 9)
                    ww = coord_eval(c, A, yy, old, n)
                    k = int(np.argmin(ww))
                    if ww[k] < bv - tol:
                        bx = float(yy[k])
                        bv = float(ww[k])
                if bv < cur - tol:
                    d = bx - old
                    c[p:p + n] += d * om
                    c[st:st + n] -= d * x
                    c[diag] -= d * d
                    x[p] = bx
                    om[diag - p] = 1.0 - bx
                    improved = True
        xp = project_simplex(x, n)
        cp = compute_c5(xp, n)
        if cp < bc - tol:
            best = xp
            bc = cp
        if not improved:
            break
    return best, bc


def binarize(h, n):
    if n % 2:
        return None
    target = n // 2
    if target <= 0 or target >= n:
        b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        b[:target] = 1.0
        return b
    idx = np.argpartition(h, target - 1)[:target]
    b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    b[idx] = 1.0
    return b


def rand_one_zero(h, n, rng):
    i = int(np.argmax(h))
    for _ in range(8):
        i = int(rng.randint(n))
        if h[i] >= 0.5:
            break
    j = int(np.argmin(h))
    for _ in range(8):
        j = int(rng.randint(n))
        if h[j] < 0.5:
            break
    if i == j:
        i = int(np.argmax(h))
        j = int(np.argmin(h))
    return i, j


def binary_trial(c, h, om, i, j, n):
    d = c.copy()
    d[i:i + n] -= om
    st = n - 1 - i
    d[st:st + n] += h
    d[j:j + n] += om
    st = n - 1 - j
    d[st:st + n] -= h
    d[n - 1] -= 2.0
    d[n - 1 + i - j] += 1.0
    d[n - 1 + j - i] += 1.0
    return d, float(d.max())


def binary_optimize(h_init, n, rng, deadline):
    b = binarize(h_init, n)
    if b is None:
        return None, np.inf
    dx = 2.0 / n
    best = project_simplex(b, n)
    bc = np.correlate(best, 1.0 - best, mode="full")
    bcm = float(bc.max())
    first = True
    stagnant = 0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        if first:
            h = best.copy()
            c = bc.copy()
            first = False
        else:
            h = best.copy()
            for _ in range(6):
                i, j = rand_one_zero(h, n, rng)
                h[i] = 0.0
                h[j] = 1.0
            c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
        cm = float(c.max())
        om = 1.0 - h[::-1]
        plateau = 0
        trials = 0
        phase_best = False
        while time.time() < deadline and plateau < 6000 and trials < 40000:
            i, j = rand_one_zero(h, n, rng)
            if i == j:
                continue
            c2, nm = binary_trial(c, h, om, i, j, n)
            trials += 1
            if nm < cm - 1e-9:
                c = c2
                cm = nm
                h[i] = 0.0
                h[j] = 1.0
                om[n - 1 - i] = 1.0
                om[n - 1 - j] = 0.0
                plateau = 0
                if cm < bcm:
                    best = h.copy()
                    bcm = cm
                    phase_best = True
            else:
                plateau += 1
        if phase_best:
            stagnant = 0
        else:
            stagnant += 1
            if stagnant > 3:
                break
    return best, bcm * dx


def transfer_h(h, n, n_tgt):
    if n_tgt == n:
        return project_simplex(h, n_tgt).copy()
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n)
    y = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_tgt)
    return project_simplex(np.interp(y, x, h), n_tgt)


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + max(5.0, budget_s - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    try:
        h0 = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = int(len(h0))
    except Exception:
        n0 = 400
        h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5)

    h0 = project_simplex(h0, n0)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_c = float(compute_c5(h0, n0))
    n_best = int(n0)

    rev = h0[::-1]
    for v in (0.5 * (h0 + rev), 0.5 * (h0 + (1.0 - rev))):
        vv = project_simplex(v, n_best)
        cv = compute_c5(vv, n_best)
        if cv < best_c:
            best_h = vv
            best_c = cv

    if n_best % 2 == 0:
        hb = binarize(h0, n_best)
        if hb is not None:
            cb = compute_c5(hb, n_best)
            if cb < best_c:
                best_h = hb
                best_c = cb

    if time.time() > deadline - 30.0:
        fh = project_simplex(best_h, n_best)
        return fh, float(compute_c5(fh, n_best)), int(n_best)

    t_cont = min(deadline - 250.0, t0 + max(40.0, 0.40 * budget_s))
    if time.time() < t_cont:
        h1, c1 = slsqp_polish(best_h, n_best, [1000, 10000, 100000, 1000000], 120, t_cont)
        if c1 < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = h1, c1
        h1, c1 = coord_cd(best_h, n_best, max_passes=6, deadline=t_cont, grid=32, refine=12)
        if c1 < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = h1, c1

    t_bin = min(deadline - 150.0, t0 + max(80.0, 0.65 * budget_s))
    if n_best % 2 == 0 and time.time() < t_bin:
        hb, cb = binary_optimize(best_h, n_best, rng, t_bin)
        if hb is not None and cb < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = hb, cb
            t_ext = min(deadline - 120.0, time.time() + 80.0)
            h1, c1 = slsqp_polish(best_h, n_best, [20000, 200000, 2000000], 100, t_ext)
            if c1 < best_c:
                best_h, best_c = h1, c1
            h1, c1 = coord_cd(best_h, n_best, max_passes=3, deadline=t_ext, grid=32, refine=12)
            if c1 < best_c:
                best_h, best_c = h1, c1

    cands = [400, 300, 600, 500, 450, 550, 350, 700]
    tried = [n_best]
    for nt in cands[:6]:
        if len(tried) >= 5:
            break
        if time.time() > deadline - 90.0:
            break
        if nt in tried or nt < 100 or nt > 1000 or nt % 2:
            continue
        tried.append(nt)
        ht = transfer_h(best_h, n_best, nt)
        cv = compute_c5(ht, nt)
        if cv < best_c:
            best_h, best_c, n_best = ht, cv, int(nt)
        chunk = min(deadline - 70.0, time.time() + 70.0)
        if time.time() < chunk:
            h1, c1 = slsqp_polish(ht, nt, [20000], 60, chunk)
            h1, c1 = coord_cd(h1, nt, max_passes=2, deadline=chunk, grid=24, refine=8)
            if c1 < best_c:
                best_h, best_c, n_best = h1, c1, int(nt)
                ht = transfer_h(best_h, n_best, nt)
            if nt % 2 == 0:
                hb, cb = binary_optimize(ht, nt, rng, chunk)
                if hb is not None and cb < best_c:
                    best_h, best_c, n_best = hb, cb, int(nt)

    tf1 = min(deadline - 30.0, time.time() + 80.0)
    if time.time() < tf1:
        h1, c1 = slsqp_polish(best_h, n_best, [200000, 2000000], 160, tf1)
        if c1 < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = h1, c1

    tf2 = min(deadline - 5.0, time.time() + 120.0)
    if time.time() < tf2:
        h1, c1 = coord_cd(best_h, n_best, max_passes=3, deadline=tf2, grid=40, refine=16)
        if c1 < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = h1, c1

    if n_best % 2 == 0 and time.time() < deadline - 10.0:
        hb, cb = binary_optimize(best_h, n_best, rng, deadline)
        if hb is not None and cb < best_c:
            best_h, best_c = hb, cb

    final_h = project_simplex(best_h, n_best)
    final_c = float(compute_c5(final_h, n_best))
    return final_h, final_c, int(n_best)
```
